Welcome back to Perilous Research, DailyMTG's exclusive [**Magic Online**](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/http://archive.wizards.com/Magic/Digital/MagicOnline.aspx?x=mtg/digital/magiconline/download) column. ***Magic** 2014* hits the shelves tomorrow night. There's a lot of speculation about the Standard metagame and how it will look once ***Magic** 2014* becomes legal. Let's take a moment to discuss the implications 

![](http://media.wizards.com/images/magic/daily/pr/pr256_liliana.jpg)  

I expect [Gladecover Scout](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gladecover+Scout) and [Fiendslayer Paladin](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fiendslayer+Paladin) to push a lot of players toward cards like [Unflinching Courage](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unflinching+Courage) and [Rancor](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rancor). As a result, cards like [Rolling Temblor](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rolling+Temblor) will probably see a significant increase in play. We can also expect [Liliana of the Veil](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+of+the+Veil), [Far amp; Away](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Far+%1Aamp%3B+Away), and [Devour Flesh](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Devour+Flesh) to become more common. 

[Archangel of Thune](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archangel+of+Thune) is my favorite card in ***Magic** 2014* . We have no way of knowing what kind of removal people will be playing, but I expect [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade) to be somewhat popular, at least out of sideboards. There are a number of ways to force the Angel through your opponent's removal, though. Cards like [Sin Collector](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sin+Collector) and [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress) seem like the best options available. I have many fond memories of [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel) and the new ***Magic** 2014* mythic rare is eerily similar in the way that it dominates a game. 

[Kalonian Hydra](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kalonian+Hydra) is one of the more impressive cards. It can't claw you back into a game like [Archangel of Thune](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archangel+of+Thune), but it closes a game faster than just about anything. The card has amazing synergy with [Exava, Rakdos Blood Witch](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exava%2C+Rakdos+Blood+Witch) and I wouldn't be surprised if we saw an aggressive/midrangey Jund strategy that took advantage of this excellent interaction. The card also combos nicely with most of the other Golgari and Rakdos creatures from *Return to Ravnica* and *Dragon's Maze*. 

<table><tbody><tr><td><div><div><a href="https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kalonian+Hydra"><img alt="" src="https://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Kalonian+Hydra"></a></div></div></td><td><div><div><a href="https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Exava%2C+Rakdos+Blood+Witch"><img alt="" src="https://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Exava%2C+Rakdos+Blood+Witch"></a></div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>

  

Today, I'd like to take a final look at pre-***Magic** 2014* Standard. I'm impressed with the current Standard's diversity from start to finish. Here we are, in the last week of the format, and there are still more than ten unique and competitive archetypes. Learning about these decks will help us lay a groundwork for our ***Magic** 2014* creations. Let's jump right in and start checking out some decklists. 

#### BLUEDEVlL's Jund Aggro/Midrange Hybrid

Conscripts";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}} 

  

This is a reasonably exciting new deck. The deck takes elements from the aggressive Jund strategies and couples them with [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells) and [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite). This puts the deck in an incredible position against most decks in the field. I really love the fact that the deck can [Burning-Tree Emissary](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burning-Tree+Emissary) into [Farseek](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Farseek). It's important to establish board presence quickly in the current Standard format and that seems like a great way to apply pressure, and put up defenses, all while ramping you into a juicy Bonfire or powerful four- or five-mana spell.

Verdict";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}} 

  

My version of Red-White-Blue is still going strong. Sammy T managed to go undefeated on Day 2 at [Grand Prix Miami](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/http://archive.wizards.com/magic/magazine/article.aspx?x=mtg/daily/eventcoverage/gpmia13/welcome) with this main deck. There's still some debate as to whether you should play a third [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite) or a third [Sphinx's Revelation](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx%27s+Revelation). I currently have the third Thundermaw and I've been very happy with it.

White-Black-Red Midrange strategies are well positioned in the current format. There are very few control decks these days and the deck murders aggressive strategies. I played this deck for a bit and found myself often flooded or unable to draw a fifth land when it was necessary. [Faithless Looting](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faithless+Looting) does a lot of work solving that problem and I expect these lists to play one to three copies consistently going forward.

Decks like this have seen a bit of resurgence over the last few weeks. The deck is very powerful, but it found itself being outraced by the Blitz decks that were popular last month. This deck has become a contender again now that Naya [Burning-Tree Emissary](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burning-Tree+Emissary) decks have mostly been scrapped in favor of lists with [Voice of Resurgence](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Voice+of+Resurgence).

Geist";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}} 

  

We can expect this deck to get even bigger once ***Magic** 2014* becomes legal and the new legend rule takes effect. [Gladecover Scout](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gladecover+Scout) gives the deck an incredible one-mana creature to dump its enchantments onto and an enchanted [Fiendslayer Paladin](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fiendslayer+Paladin) is an impossible-to-race [Geist of Saint Traft](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geist+of+Saint+Traft) against the red-based aggressive decks. Make sure you bring a lot of hate for this deck if you don't plan on playing it yourself. [Ray of Revelation](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ray+of+Revelation) is a very good card; don't leave yours at home.

[Angel of Glory's Rise](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Glory%27s+Rise) made a comeback in the last month as players started taking graveyard hate out of their decks. The newest versions of the deck don't bother using [Undercity Informer](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Undercity+Informer) to "go infinite" and win the game that turn. There aren't enough Esper decks in the field that can actually grind you out once you combo anymore. Having the loop with a [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells) or even double [Fiend Hunter](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fiend+Hunter) is almost always going to be good enough. Try leaving an Angel exiled with [Fiend Hunter](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fiend+Hunter) to protect yourself from a big [Bonfire of the Damned](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonfire+of+the+Damned) in the appropriate matchups and comboing will almost always result in victory. 

<table><tbody><tr><td>[card]Angel of Glory's Rise[/card]</td><td>[card]Fiend Hunter[/card]</td></tr></tbody></table>

Midrange Naya decks went from being a dominant force to practically being nonexistent over the last two weeks. This deck has a lot going for it, but the new wave of Red-White-Blue Flash decks and Jund's resurgence have pushed it out of the limelight for the time being.

#### sai\_2011's Bant Tempo

Courage";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}} 

  

I haven't seen this deck in a while, but I'm very aware of its potential power. I played against a similar deck in the semifinals of the PTQ I won and I had two separate turns where my opponent was one pump spell away from killing me for the match win. I have tested this strategy and I found myself underwhelmed with its consistency. That being said, this version has a lot more creatures and a lot less gimmicks. This version of the deck seems very strong against non-[Burning-Tree Emissary](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burning-Tree+Emissary) decks.

Helix";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}} 

  

Act 2 Aristocrats is still going strong in the last week of *Dragon's Maze* Standard. The deck isn't as powerful as it once was and it seems players are taking some serious risks by playing cards like [Cavern of Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cavern+of+Souls) in their main deck. That being said, the deck has some of the most powerful draws in the format and most cards in the deck combo with at least two other cards. I could see myself playing this with at least five cards for hexproof in my sideboard in my first post-***Magic** 2014* Standard event. 

<table><tbody><tr><td>[card]Cavern of Souls[/card]</td><td>[card]Falkenrath Aristocrat[/card]</td></tr></tbody></table>

We've all seen this deck by now, but it's worth noting that the Golgari deck was not a flavor of the week or metagame call. The deck has real power and it performs very well in the current Standard format. It kills everything and then dominates the game with a [Desecration Demon](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Desecration+Demon).



  

Grixis Control is a newer strategy that was initially popularized by Gerard Fabiano and brought out of obscurity by Joel Larsson. I'm still on the fence with this deck and its lack of [Sphinx's Revelation](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphinx%27s+Revelation), but it seems like it has what it takes to do well in a format where a lot of players don't have the big instant. The deck can easily change its removal suite to include [Devour Flesh](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Devour+Flesh) and [Liliana of the Veil](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+of+the+Veil) once ***Magic** 2014* becomes legal; this would allow the deck to demolish Hexproof, a deck we can expect to skyrocket in popularity. Let's see if a Grixis Control strategy can become the new metagame deck of choice in response to the incoming wave of [Gladecover Scouts](https://web.archive.org/web/20130718000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gladecover+Scouts).
